From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F43.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m having a hard time staying with what I’m doing at work. On the floor, I’ll be dealing with a patient flow issue, and then an IV pump beeps or a call light goes off, or someone asks another nurse something… and I just lose the thread. I forget what I was in the middle of. It’s been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Is this difficulty with focusing and remembering what you were doing happening only at work or also in other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: It’s not just work. It happens at home too… like I’ll mean to call someone back, or pay a bill, or I’ll set a reminder for an appointment and still somehow miss it. Even routine things just kind of slip.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed when these memory lapses happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I get frustrated, definitely. Overwhelmed maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. It’s more like, “how did I forget that again?” and then I try to move on.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these memory lapses and difficulties with focusing?\n\nPatient: It’s been… probably close to two years now. It got a lot more noticeable after I took on the expanded charge-nurse responsibilities. Since then it’s felt like a major problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or depressed when these memory lapses happen?\n\nPatient: Not really sad, no. Mostly just annoyed with myself… maybe embarrassed sometimes. I don’t think depressed is the word I’d use.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I wasn’t really the kid who was constantly getting up, but in elementary school teachers would write that I was bright but careless. I lost worksheets a lot, forgot to turn in homework… that kind of thing. My mom had to sit with me most nights to keep me on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting, tapping your hands or feet, or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any obvious way. I might shift around a little, but I haven’t really noticed tapping or feeling like I can’t sit still.\n\nDoctor: Were you ever described as being overly active or having a lot of excess energy when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don’t remember being described that way. I was more… distracted, I guess, than overly energetic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I can get into something sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say I tune everything out. I haven’t paid much attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever become so absorbed in an activity that you lose track of time or forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really notice that happening, or at least I can’t think of a clear example.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant improvement in your ability to focus when you're doing something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe a little sometimes, if it’s something I actually want to do, but I haven’t really noticed a big difference.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activities where you become so focused that you don't notice anything else around you?\n\nPatient: Not really… I can’t think of anything specific where I’m that locked in. Maybe I just don’t notice it, but nothing comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming extremely focused on a task to the point where nothing else matters?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, not to that level. I can focus a bit more if something’s interesting, but “nothing else matters” doesn’t really sound like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself focusing on something so intensely that you don't even notice other people talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. If someone’s talking to me, I usually notice, even if I’m distracted. I can’t think of a time where I was that absorbed.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focusing and remembering what you were doing significantly impacted your relationships with friends, family, or colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at work, definitely. It’s gotten serious enough that I’m on formal supervision now, and some of the other nurses have to double-check parts of my follow-through when I’m charge. That’s been… hard. It makes me feel like I’m becoming more of a liability than a leader.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant differences in your ability to focus when you are in a quiet, distraction-free environment versus a busy, noisy one?\n\nPatient: Yes. In a quiet space it’s not as bad.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I wasn’t really the kid who was constantly getting up, but in elementary school teachers would write that I was bright but careless. I lost worksheets a lot, forgot to turn in homework… that kind of thing. My mom had to sit with me most nights to keep me on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting, tapping your hands or feet, or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any obvious way. I might shift around a little, but I haven’t really noticed tapping or feeling like I can’t sit still.\n\nDoctor: Were you ever described as being overly active or having a lot of excess energy when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don’t remember being described that way. I was more… distracted, I guess, than overly energetic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I can get into something sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say I tune everything out. I haven’t paid much attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever become so absorbed in an activity that you lose track of time or forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really notice that happening, or at least I can’t think of a clear example.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant improvement in your ability to focus when you're doing something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe a little sometimes, if it’s something I actually want to do, but I haven’t really noticed a big difference.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any activities where you become so focused that you don't notice anything else around you?\n\nPatient: Not really… I can’t think of anything specific where I’m that locked in. Maybe I just don’t notice it, but nothing comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming extremely focused on a task to the point where nothing else matters?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, not to that level. I can focus a bit more if something’s interesting, but “nothing else matters” doesn’t really sound like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself focusing on something so intensely that you don't even notice other people talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. If someone’s talking to me, I usually notice, even if I’m distracted. I can’t think of a time where I was that absorbed.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focusing and remembering what you were doing significantly impacted your relationships with friends, family, or colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at work, definitely. It’s gotten serious enough that I’m on formal supervision now, and some of the other nurses have to double-check parts of my follow-through when I’m charge. That’s been… hard. It makes me feel like I’m becoming more of a liability than a leader.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant differences in your ability to focus when you are in a quiet, distraction-free environment versus a busy, noisy one?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s definitely worse when it’s busy or noisy. On the floor, if an IV pump beeps, or a call light goes off, or someone asks another nurse something nearby, I just… lose what I was doing. Like I’ll be working through a patient flow issue and suddenly it’s gone. In a quiet space it’s not as bad.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific tasks or activities where you feel you can focus without any difficulty?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Nothing really comes to mind as something I can count on being easy to focus on. I guess if it’s very straightforward and quiet, maybe, but… I can’t think of a specific activity.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focusing and remembering what you were doing have caused you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. Mostly because of work. Being put on formal supervision and having other nurses double-check my follow-through when I’m charge… it’s embarrassing. It makes me feel like I’m not leading, like I’m becoming a liability.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m having a hard time staying with what I’m doing at work. On the floor, I’ll be dealing with a patient flow issue, and then an IV pump beeps or a call light goes off, or someone asks another nurse something… and I just lose the thread. I forget what I was in the middle of. It’s been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Is this difficulty with focusing and remembering what you were doing happening only at work or also in other parts of your life?\n\nPatient: It’s not just work. It happens at home too… like I’ll mean to call someone back, or pay a bill, or I’ll set a reminder for an appointment and still somehow miss it. Even routine things just kind of slip.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or overwhelmed when these memory lapses happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I get frustrated, definitely. Overwhelmed maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it anxious exactly. It’s more like, “how did I forget that again?” and then I try to move on.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these memory lapses and difficulties with focusing?\n\nPatient: It’s been… probably close to two years now. It got a lot more noticeable after I took on the expanded charge-nurse responsibilities. Since then it’s felt like a major problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or depressed when these memory lapses happen?\n\nPatient: Not really sad, no. Mostly just annoyed with myself… maybe embarrassed sometimes. I don’t think depressed is the word I’d use.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble paying attention or sitting still when you were a kid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I wasn’t really the kid who was constantly getting up, but in elementary school teachers would write that I was bright but careless. I lost worksheets a lot, forgot to turn in homework… that kind of thing. My mom had to sit with me most nights to keep me on track.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting, tapping your hands or feet, or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any obvious way. I might shift around a little, but I haven’t really noticed tapping or feeling like I can’t sit still.\n\nDoctor: Were you ever described as being overly active or having a lot of excess energy when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I don’t remember being described that way. I was more… distracted, I guess, than overly energetic.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I can get into something sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say I tune everything out. I haven’t paid much attention to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever become so absorbed in an activity that you lose track of time or forget about everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t really notice that happening, or at least I can’t think of a clear example.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant improvement in your ability to focus when you're doing something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Maybe I just don’t notice it, but nothing comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself becoming extremely focused on a task to the point where nothing else matters?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, not to that level. I can focus a bit more if something’s interesting, but “nothing else matters” doesn’t really sound like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself focusing on something so intensely that you don't even notice other people talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. If someone’s talking to me, I usually notice, even if I’m distracted. I can’t think of a time where I was that absorbed.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focusing and remembering what you were doing significantly impacted your relationships with friends, family, or colleagues?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at work, definitely. It’s gotten serious enough that I’m on formal supervision now, and some of the other nurses have to double-check parts of my follow-through when I’m charge. That’s been… hard. It makes me feel like I’m becoming more of a liability than a leader.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant differences in your ability to focus when you are in a quiet, distraction-free environment versus a busy, noisy one?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s definitely worse when it’s busy or noisy. On the floor, if an IV pump beeps, or a call light goes off, or someone asks another nurse something nearby, I just… lose what I was doing. Like I’ll be working through a patient flow issue and suddenly it’s gone. In a quiet space it’s not as bad.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific tasks or activities where you feel you can focus without any difficulty?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. Nothing really comes to mind as something I can count on being easy to focus on. I guess if it’s very straightforward and quiet, maybe, but… I can’t think of a specific activity.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focusing and remembering what you were doing have caused you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. Mostly because of work. Being put on formal supervision and having other nurses double-check my follow-through when I’m charge… it’s embarrassing. It makes me feel like I’m not leading, like I’m becoming a liability.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever become so focused on something that you can't think of anything else?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can get caught up in something for a little while, but I wouldn’t say it’s like I can’t think of anything else. I haven’t really noticed that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
